.

Appendix of “Computationally Efficient and Statistically Optimal Robust High-Dimensional Linear Regression”

Yinan Shen, Jingyang Li, Jian-Feng Cai and Dong Xia

Hong Kong University of Science and Technology

Section
A
presents materials that are not shown in the main text due to limited length, including Riemannian optimization details and simulation results. In Section
B
, we present proofs of sparse vector recovery. Section
C
proves Proposition
1
, convergence dynamics of low-rank regression under two-phase regularity conditions. Section
D
contains four sub-sections,
D.1
-
D.4
, which verify the regularity conditions in certain different cases, absolute loss, Huber loss and quantile loss. The initialization analysis is provided in Section
E
. Then Section
F
exhibits the technical lemmas. Finally, lengthy proofs of two significant lemmas
14
matrix perturbation theories and Proposition
3
empirical processes for low-rank regression are put in Section
G
and Section
H
respectively. In the last section
I
, we analyze outlier robustness.

The following key ingredients act as a guidance for the proof of low-rank linear regression.

1.

Tangent Space over
{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
.
In the algorithm, it updates in the way of
{\mathbf{T}}_{l+1}=\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{T}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l}))
. Then in the convergence analyses, it involves the upper bound of
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
and
\left|\langle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{G}}_{l},{\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}\rangle\right|
. As for the first term, note that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}+{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}){\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}

could be written into sum of two rank at most
r
matrices. Then we use ‘Partial Frobenius Norm’ (see
Tong et al. 2021a
or Lemma
12
) to bound
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F}}
; see Lemma
12
and Lemma
13
for more details. As for the second term, we use

\displaystyle\left|\langle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{G}}_{l},{\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}\rangle\right|=\left|\langle{\mathbf{G}}_{l},{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*})\rangle\right|,

and use
{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*})=-({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}){\mathbf{T}}^{*}({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})=({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*})({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})
. Hence, with definition of partial Frobenius norm Lemma
12
, one has

\displaystyle\left|\langle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{G}}_{l},{\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}\rangle\right|\leq\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*})({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

2.

Perturbation lemma.
Based on Theorem 1 in work
Xia 2021
, we develop powerful analysis scheme for matrix perturbations, where we have
\|{\mathbf{T}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}+O(\|{\mathbf{T}}_{l}-{\mathbf{T}}^{*}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2})
. For clarity, we present Theorem 1 in work
Xia 2021
as Theorem
11
. Lemma
18
presents symmetric matrix perturbations, based on which we have general matrix perturbations Lemma
14
.

3.

Empirical Processes.
We use emprical process theories
[
Ludoux and Talagrand 1991
,
Van Der Vaart et al. 1996
,
Adamczak 2008
]
to prove the regularity properties. One of the merits is that it enables strong exponential probability.

4.

Initialization Guarantees.
Note that the perturbation bound and convergence dynamics Equation (
9
) require
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{r}
. Thus we start with a warm initialization. A clean and simple spectral initialization scheme is provided. Section
E
shows the proofs.

5.

Proposition 1.
Proposition
1
analyzes the general convergence under two-phase regularity property assumption and depicts the stepsize selections. Lemma
4
,
6
,
8
and
10
prove two-phase regularity properties under several settings. Namely, the loss function behaves in the typical non-smooth way when it is far from the oracle matrix
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
, while when it gets sufficiently close to
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
, the loss function performs alike smooth
\ell_{2}
loss.

Appendix A
Additional Results

This section collects some results which are not presented in the main context due to the page limit.

A.1
Computational benefit of Riemannian sub-gradient algorithm

The Riemannian sub-gradient is fast computable. Let
{\mathbf{M}}_{l}={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}
be the thin SVD of
{\mathbf{M}}_{l}
. It is well-known
[
Absil et al. 2009
,
Vandereycken 2013
]
that the tangent space
{\mathbb{T}}_{l}
can be characterized by
{\mathbb{T}}_{l}:=\{{\mathbf{Z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}:{\mathbf{Z}}={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times r},{\mathbf{L}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times r}\}
. Then projection of
{\mathbf{G}}_{l}
onto
{\mathbb{T}}_{l}
is

{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}+{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top},

which is of rank at most
2r
. The first step in Algorithm
2
could be written as

\displaystyle{~~~~}{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{l}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}\right){\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}-\eta_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\left({\mathbf{I}}_{d_{2}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}\right)-\eta_{l}\left({\mathbf{I}}_{d_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}\right){\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}.

Let
\left({\mathbf{I}}_{d_{2}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}\right){\mathbf{G}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}={\mathbf{Q}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}
and
\left({\mathbf{I}}_{d_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}\right){\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}={\mathbf{Q}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}
be the QR decomposition. Then the update is

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})=\left[{\mathbf{U}}_{l}\;{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}&-\eta_{l}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}\\
-\eta_{l}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}&\boldsymbol{0}\end{matrix}\right]\left[{\mathbf{V}}_{l}\;{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\right]^{\top},

with
\left[{\mathbf{U}}_{l}\;{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\right]
,
\left[{\mathbf{V}}_{l}\;{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\right]
being orthogonal matrices. Consequently, the final step of retraction only requires the SVD of the medium term, a
2r\times 2r
matrix, which can be computed using
O(r^{3})
floating point operations. See, e.g.,
Vandereycken 2013
and
Mishra et al. 2014
for more details.

A.2
More simulation results of low-rank linear regression

Accuracy

We now compare the statistical accuracy of the final estimator output by RsGrad, ScaledSM and those by convex approaches including the nuclear norm penalized
[
Elsener and van de Geer 2018
]
absolute loss (convex
\ell_{1}
), Huber loss (convex Huber) and the square loss (
\ell_{2}
-loss) by seminal work
Candes and Plan 2011
. Functions from Matlab library cvx
[
Grant and Boyd 2014
]
are borrowed to implement the convex methods for convex
\ell_{1}
, convex Huber and
\ell_{2}
-loss. For each setting, every method is repeated for
10
times and the box-plots of error rates are presented.

The parameter settings are similar as above where both the Gaussian noise and Student’s
t
-distribution are experimented. The comparison of statistical accuracy under Gaussian noise is displayed in Figure
5
. The top two plots suggest that all the convex methods and ScaledSM performs poorly when sample size is small (
n=2.5rd_{1}
). On the other hand, ScaledSM becomes much better when sample size is large but still under-performs RsGrad, as predicted by our theory. Similar results are also observed when noise has a Students’
t
-distribution with d.f.
\nu=2
. See Figure
6
.

From Figure
5
and Figure
6
, we observe that non-convex models achieve better accuracy, which may be attributed to more finely tuning parameters than convex approaches. Convex ones we compare with are also proven statistically optimal in theory and are computationally more demanding. For instance, in the case
n=5rd_{1}
, convex
\ell_{1}
, convex Huber, convex
\ell_{2}
need more than 1.5h, 1.5h, 3h to complete the ten repetitions, respectively. In sharp contrast, RsGrad and ScaledSM only take around ten minutes. When it comes to an even larger sample size like
n=10d_{1}r
, the Matlab package on our computing platform needs 3h and 6h to complete the simulations (10 repetitions) for convex
\ell_{1}
and convex Huber, respectively, whereas RsGrad and ScaledSM each takes about 20 minutes to do the job.

Appendix B
Proofs of Sparse Linear Regression

In general, there are three key technical points, Proposition
2
, Theorem
9
and Lemma
2
; see below.

Proposition 2
.

Suppose function
f(\cdot)
is given in Equation (
3
). Then there exist absolute constants
C,C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}\left[f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right]\right|\leq C\sqrt{nsC_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/s)}\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}

holds for all
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\left\{{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\;\left|\text{supp}({\mathbf{b}})\right|\leq s\right\}
with probability exceeding
1-\exp(-\frac{C_{1}^{2}s\log(2d/s)}{3})-3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n\log(2d/s)}}{\log n}\right)
.

Proposition
2
bounds deviation of
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. Its proof is based on empirical process theories and is presented later in this section. Besides, we also need perturbation bound. Rough perturbation bound
\|{\mathcal{H}}_{K}({\mathbf{b}})-{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}\leq 2\|{\mathbf{b}}-{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}
is usually used in
\ell_{2}
loss
[
Blumensath and Davies 2009
,
Blumensath and Davies 2010
]
and is good enough to derive statiscal optimality in
\ell_{2}
loss. However, the coefficient
2
is too large for phase two convergence of
\ell_{1}
loss. Thanksfully, work
Shen and Li 2017
provides a tight bound for hard thresholding perturbations.

Theorem 9

(Tight Bound for Hard Thresholding
Shen and Li 2017
)
.

Let
{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be an arbitrary vector and
{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be any
K
-sparse signal. For any
k\geq K
, we have the following bound:

\left\|\mathcal{H}_{k}(\boldsymbol{b})-\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\nu}\|\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{x}\|_{2},\quad\nu=1+\frac{\rho+\sqrt{(4+\rho)\rho}}{2},\quad\rho=\frac{\min\{K,d-k\}}{k-K+\min\{K,d-k\}}.

Note that to some extent larger hard thresholding parameter
k
infers smaller upper bound coefficient
\nu
. Besides, Theorem
9
works for arbitrary
{\mathbf{b}}
and puts no assumption on
{\mathbf{b}}
. It leads to we do not require good initializations for sparse vector recovery but need the practical thresholding parameter
\tilde{s}\geq\min\{cs,d\}
to have convergence guarantees. We conjecture there are tighter bounds if with small enough
\|{\mathbf{b}}-{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}
based on which we could choose
\tilde{s}=s
. We leave this for future study.

Lemma 2
.

Suppose function
f(\cdot)
is given in Equation (
3
). Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then for any vector
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\left\{{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\,\|{\mathbf{b}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq\frac{n}{6b_{0}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

For arbitrary
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2})\leq nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\cdot\left[\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\right].

Lemma
2
provides upper bound and lower bound of expectation of
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2})
and
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
, respectively. Then we are ready to prove regularity proporties Lemma
1
.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

We shall assume the event

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}:=\left\{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\;|\text{supp}(\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\leq 3\tilde{s}}\left|f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}\left[f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right]\right|\cdot\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}^{-1}\leq C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\right\}

holds. Proposition
2
proves
{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}})\geq 1-\exp(-\frac{C^{2}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{3})-3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n\log(2d/\tilde{s})}}{\log n}\right)
. Based on event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we shall obtain the regularity properties.

First, analyze phase one properties, namely, when
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. As for
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
, under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, it has

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbf{X}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\|_{1}-\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\|_{1}\right]-C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\|_{1}-2{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\|_{1}-C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

Notice that
\left|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|
follows folded Gaussian distributions and then we have
{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\|_{1}\geq n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}
. Thus, together with
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|=\gamma
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
and
n\geq C_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})
, it has

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq\frac{n}{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

Then consider
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}
. By definition of sub-gradient, it has

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}^{2}=\langle{\mathbf{G}},{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle\leq f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}).

(5)

On the other hand, under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, it has

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})+C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{1}+C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}.

Then together with folded Gaussian distributions, it has
{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{1}\leq n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}
. More details of calculations can be found in Corollary
2
. Furthermore, with
n\geq C_{1}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})
, it has

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}.

Then the above equation and Equation (
5
) lead to

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}.

Secondly, analyze phase two properties, when
C_{2}b_{0}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{n}}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. With Lemma
2
and event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{6b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-C\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2},

where the last line is from
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})+C_{1}\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\cdot\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{nC_{{\tiny u}}}{b_{1}}\left[\left(\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\right)^{2}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}^{2}+2\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~}+C_{1}\sqrt{n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\cdot\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b_{1}}{4nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}^{2}+(2+C_{1})\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2},

where the second equation is from Lemma
2
and the last equation is from the phase two region constraint
\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{C_{{\tiny u}}n}}b_{1}\leq C_{2}b_{0}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{n}}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}
. Meanwhile, by definition of sub-gradient, we obtain a lower bound for
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\geq\langle{\mathbf{G}},\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle=\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Combine the above two equations and then the quadratic inequality leads to

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq C_{3}C_{{\tiny u}}\frac{n}{b_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

∎

Then we are ready to prove convergence dynamics of IHT-
\ell_{1}
, Theorem
1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

In phase one, for convenience, denote
D_{l}:=(1-c_{1})^{l}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}
. Prove by induction and it holds obviously for
l=0
. We suppose
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq D_{l}
and we are going to prove
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq D_{l+1}
. We have

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle+\eta_{l}^{2}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\left(f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right)+\eta_{l}^{2}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\eta_{l}\frac{n}{2}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}+n^{2}\eta_{l}^{2}C_{{\tiny u}},

where the first line is due to
\text{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\subseteq\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}
that
\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle=\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle
, the second line uses sub-gradient definition and the last line uses Lemma
1
.
By induction, we have
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq D_{l}
and selection of stepsize says
\eta_{l}\in n^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}}\cdot[\frac{1}{8}D_{l},\frac{3}{8}D_{l}]
. Based on quadratic function properties Claim
1
, we have

\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\eta_{l}\frac{n}{2}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq D_{l}^{2}-\eta_{l}\frac{n}{2}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}D_{l}.

Then insert stepsize selection and with quadratic function properties, it becomes

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{64}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{C_{{\tiny u}}}\right)\cdot D_{l}^{2}.

It implies

\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{128}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{C_{{\tiny u}}}\right)D_{l}.

Then with hard thresholding perturbation Theorem
9
and
\tilde{s}
selection, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}=\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\tilde{s}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l}))-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{64}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{C_{{\tiny u}}}\right)D_{l}\leq(1-c_{1})D_{l}=D_{l+1},

where constant
c_{1}\leq\frac{1}{64}\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}}
.

Similarly, in phase two, based on Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle+\eta_{l}^{2}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\left(f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right)+\eta_{l}^{2}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\eta_{l}\frac{n}{6b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+C_{3}^{2}n^{2}b_{1}^{-2}\eta_{l}^{2}C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

With stepsize
\eta_{l}\in\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{b_{0}}\cdot[C_{4},C_{5}]
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1-\tilde{c}_{2}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}{C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}\right)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

It leads to

\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-\eta_{l}{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{l}\cup\Pi_{l}\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\tilde{c}_{2}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}\right)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

Then with
\tilde{s}\geq\min\left\{C_{6}s\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}^{8}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{8}}\cdot\frac{b_{0}^{8}}{b_{1}^{8}},d\right\}
, perturbation bound Theorem
9
infers

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{s}{\tilde{s}}+\sqrt{\frac{s}{\tilde{s}}}}\right)\cdot\left(1-\tilde{c}_{2}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}\right)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-c_{2}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}{C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}\right)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
2
, Vector Recovery Empirical Process.

Denote
{\mathbb{V}}_{s}:=\left\{{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\;\left|\text{supp}({\mathbf{b}})\right|\leq s\right\}
. Then consider

\displaystyle Z:=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\;\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{s}}\left|f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}\left[f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right]\right|\big/\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}.

With
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-Y_{i}|
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}Z

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\;\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{s}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\left(|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-Y_{i}|-|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-Y_{i}|\right)\right|\big/\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{s}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right|\big/\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=4{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{s}}\left|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)^{\top}\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right|\big/\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{|\Omega|\leq s}\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}.

where
\{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is Rademacher sequence independent of
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
and operator
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega}(\cdot)
only keeps entries in
\Omega
. The first inequality is from Theorem
12
and the second inequality is from Theorem
13
.

Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\sim N(\boldsymbol{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i})
and
\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\|\leq nC_{{\tiny u}}
and then by Corollary
1
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{|\Omega|\leq s}\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\left({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{|\Omega|\leq s}\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{nsC_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/s)}
. Thus,
{\mathbb{E}}Z
could be upper bounded with

{\mathbb{E}}Z\leq C\sqrt{nsC_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/s)}.

With similar analyses in matrix case Section
H
, we have

\displaystyle~~~\left\|\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\;\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{s}}|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-Y_{i}|-|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-Y_{i}|-{\mathbb{E}}\left[|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-Y_{i}|-|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-Y_{i}|\right]\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}s}\log n,

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-Y_{i}|-|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-Y_{i}|\right)^{2}\big/\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{{\tiny u}}.

Thus with Theorem
14
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z\geq C\sqrt{nsC_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/s)}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}s\log(2d/s)}{3}\right)+3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n\log(2d/s)}}{\log n}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2
.

Notice that
{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
has the following expansion

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-{\mathbb{E}}\left|\xi_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]\right].

To bound the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]
, we shall first analyze
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi-t_{0}|
, with
t_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi-t_{0}|&=\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+\int_{s<t_{0}}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)\\
&=2\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)\\
&=2\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+t_{0}-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s),\end{split}

(6)

where the last line is due to

\displaystyle\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)

\displaystyle=-\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,d(1-H_{\xi}(s))

\displaystyle=-(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))\bigg|_{t_{0}}^{+\infty}+\int_{s\geq t_{0}}\left(1-H_{\xi}(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))+\int_{s\geq t_{0}}\left(1-H_{\xi}(s)\right)\,ds,

whose first term vanishes

\displaystyle 0\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(\xi>s)\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})\cdot\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}}{s^{1+\varepsilon}}=0.

Set
t_{0}=\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle
and then Equation (
6
) becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]=2\int_{s\geq\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}sdH_{\xi}(s).

With
t_{0}=0
, it becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|=2\int_{s\geq 0}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}sdH_{\xi}(s).

Thus, by taking difference of the above two equations, we have the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}(H_{\xi}(s)-H_{\xi}(0))\,ds

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)\,dw\,ds,

where
H_{\xi}(0)=1/2
is used. Note that
\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}))
. Denote
z_{i}:=\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle
,
\sigma_{i}^{2}:=({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
and let
f_{z_{i}}(\cdot)
be density of
z_{i}
. Take expectation over
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on each side of the above equation and then we obtain,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\right]=2\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dw\,ds\,dt.

Note that with Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{+\sigma_{i}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dw\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{b_{0}}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{+\sigma_{i}}\int_{0}^{t}sf_{z_{i}}(t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{0}}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{+\sigma_{i}}t^{2}f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{b_{0}}\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-t^{2}/2)\,dt\geq\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{6b_{0}},

where the last line uses
\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-t^{2}/2)\,dt\geq\frac{1}{6}
. Besides, Assumption
1
implies
\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
. Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{6b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

On the other hand for arbitrary
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, based on Equation (
6
) it has

\displaystyle{~~~~}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)\,dw\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{b_{1}}\left|\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle}\int_{0}^{s}\,dw\,ds\right|

\displaystyle=b_{1}^{-1}\cdot\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\rangle^{2}+2\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\rangle\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|,

where the third line is from noise density condition, Assumption
2
. Then take expectation over
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on each side of the above equation and it becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq b_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\cdot\left[\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\right],

where Holder’s inequality is used. Sum over
i=1,\dots,n
and it is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2})\leq nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\cdot\left[\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\right],

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3

(Proposition E.1 of
Bellec et al. 2018
)
.

Let
g_{1},\ldots,g_{p}
be zero-mean Gaussian random variables with variance at most
\sigma^{2}
. Denote by
\left(g_{1}^{\sharp},\ldots,g_{p}^{\sharp}\right)
be a non-increasing rearrangement of
\left(\left|g_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|g_{p}\right|\right)
. Then

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{s\sigma^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\left(g_{j}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}>t\log\left(\frac{2p}{s}\right)\right)\leq\left(\frac{2p}{s}\right)^{1-\frac{3t}{8}}

for all
t>0
and
s\in\{1,\ldots,p\}
.

Corollary 1

(Corollary of Lemma
3
)
.

Under same conditions of Lemma
3
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\leq Cs\log(p/s)\sigma^{2},

where
C
is some absolute constant.

Proof.

We have,

\displaystyle{~~~~}{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\cdot 1_{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\leq 8s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}}\right]+\sum_{j=8}^{+\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\cdot 1_{js\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\leq(j+1)s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}+\sum_{j=8}^{+\infty}(j+1)s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}>js\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 8s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}+s\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}\cdot\sum_{j=8}^{+\infty}(j+1)\left(\left(\frac{s}{2p}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)^{j},

where the last line is from Lemma
3
and
1-\frac{3}{8}t\leq-\frac{1}{4}t
when
t\geq 8
. Also, note that
\left(\frac{s}{2p}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq 0.5^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq 0.85
and then with mathematical analyses, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=8}^{+\infty}(j+1)\left(\left(\frac{s}{2p}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)^{j}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}(j+1)\cdot(0.85)^{j}\leq\frac{1}{(1-0.85)^{2}}\leq 49.

Thus there exist some absolute constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(g_{i}^{\sharp}\right)^{2}\leq Cs\log(2p/s)\sigma^{2}.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
1

Phase One

For convenience, denote
d_{l}:=\left(1-c\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}\right)^{l}\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
. Prove by induction. When
l=0
,
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq d_{0}
is obvious. Suppose we already have
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq d_{l}
and we are going to prove
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq d_{l+1}
.

Notice that
{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}=\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l}))
and we shall use Lemma
14
to bound the distance between
{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}
and the ground truth matrix
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
. First consider
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
,

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&{~~~~~~~~~}=\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle+\eta_{l}^{2}\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(7)

By definition of sub-gradient, the second term can be bounded in the way of

\begin{split}\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle&=\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle-\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle\\
&\geq f({\mathbf{M}}_{l})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle.\\
\end{split}

(8)

Notice that
\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}(\mathbf{G}_{l})\rangle
has the following expansion, see Lemma 4.1 of
Wei et al. 2016
for more detailed calculations,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}),\mathbf{G}_{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{M}}^{*}),\mathbf{G}_{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}),{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle.

Besides, the term
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})=-({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}){\mathbf{M}}^{*}({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})
has rank at most
r
, due to
\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\leq r
. Therefore by properties of partial Frobenius norm Lemma
12
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}),{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\|({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top})({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top})\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

Furthermore, Lemma
14
shows perturbation bound
\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{8\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}}
, so the above equation becomes

\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle\leq 8\frac{1}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

Also, Condition
1
assumes
\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}
and then the above equation becomes

\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle\leq 8\frac{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Thus Equation (
8
) has the lower bound

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\rangle\geq f({\mathbf{M}}_{l})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-8\frac{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Then analyze last term of Equation (
7
),
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
. Though

{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})={\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{G}}_{l}+{\mathbf{G}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{l}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}{\mathbf{V}}_{l}^{\top}

is not
\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})
, since
{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})
could be written into sum of two rank at most
r
matrices,
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}
can be upper bounded by
\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}
up to constant scales. Lemma
13
shows

\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

Then with phase one regularity conditions in Condition
1
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}},\quad f({\mathbf{M}}_{l})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

Equation (
7
) could be bounded in the way of

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\\
&{~~~~~~~~}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+16\eta_{l}\frac{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\eta_{l}^{2}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}\\
&{~~~~~~~~}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+2\eta_{l}^{2}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2},\end{split}

(9)

where the last line uses the initialization condition and the induction that
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq d_{l}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{16}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}}\sigma_{r}
.

Claim 1
.

For quadratic function
q(x)=x^{2}-ax:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
with parameter
a>0
and for any
x_{2}\geq x_{1}\geq 0
, if
q(x_{2})>0
, then we have

q(x_{1})\leq q(x_{2}).

Proof of Claim
1
.

On the one hand, if
q(x_{1})\leq 0
, then
q(x_{1})\leq 0<q(x_{2})
. On the other hand, if
q(x_{1})\geq 0
, then it infers
x_{1}>a
. Notice that
q(\cdot)
is an increasing function when restricted to
(a/2,+\infty)
. Thus, the relation
x_{1}\leq x_{2}
implies
q(x_{1})\leq q(x_{2})
.
∎

Regard
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
as quadratic function of
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
. By induction, one already has
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq d_{l}
and by stepsize selection, we know
\eta_{l}
satisfies

\frac{1}{5}d_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{-2}\leq\eta_{l}=(1-c\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}/L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2})^{l}\cdot\eta_{0}\leq\frac{3}{10}d_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{-2}.

Thus, the quadratic function is positive at point
d_{l}
, namely
d_{l}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}d_{l}>0
. Then Claim
1
implies

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq d_{l}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}d_{l}.

As a consequence, Equation (
9
) could be bounded with

\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}&\leq d_{l}^{2}-\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}d_{l}+2\eta_{l}^{2}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}\\
&\leq(1-\frac{3}{25}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})d_{l}^{2},\end{split}

(10)

where the last line is from
\frac{1}{5}d_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{-2}\leq\eta_{l}\leq\frac{3}{10}d_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{-2}
. Take square root on each side of Equation (
10
),

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq(1-\frac{3}{50}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})d_{l}<d_{0}\leq\sigma_{r}/8.

(11)

Then apply matrix perturbation bound Lemma
14
,

\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}&=\|{\rm SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}(\mathbf{G}_{l}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},\end{split}

(12)

Insert Equation (
11
) into Equation (
12
) :

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{3}{50}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})d_{l}+\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}(1-\frac{3}{50}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})^{2}d_{l}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{1}{25}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})d_{l},

which uses
\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}d_{l}\leq\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}d_{0}\leq\frac{1}{50}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}
. That is

\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq(1-\frac{1}{25}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}})d_{l}\leq\left(1-c\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}^{2}}\right)d_{l}=d_{l+1},

with constant
c\leq\frac{1}{25}
. Hence, analyses of phase one convergence finish.

Phase Two

Consider the second phase, when
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}<\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}
. The derivation is slightly similar to phase one case,

\displaystyle{~~~~}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}(\mathbf{G}_{l})\rangle+\eta_{l}^{2}\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}(\mathbf{G}_{l})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-2\eta_{l}(f({\mathbf{M}}_{l})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*}))+2\eta_{l}\frac{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{3}+2\eta_{l}^{2}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-2\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\eta_{l}\frac{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{3}+2\eta_{l}^{2}L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{7}{4}\eta_{l}\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\eta_{l}^{2}L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

where the fourth line follows from Condition
1
that
f({\mathbf{M}}_{l})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
,
\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
and the last line uses
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{8}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}\sigma_{r}
.
Take stepsize
\frac{1}{8}\cdot\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}\leq\eta_{l}\leq\frac{3}{4}\cdot\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}
and one has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq(1-\frac{1}{8}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Take the square root of the above equation and it arrives at

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq(1-\frac{1}{16}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}<\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sigma_{r}/8.

Then use Lemma
14
to cope with the matrix perturbations,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l+1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\|{\rm SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta_{l}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}_{l}}(\mathbf{G}_{l}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-\eta{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{1}{16}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+\frac{40}{\sigma_{r}}(1-\frac{1}{8}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}})^{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{1}{32}\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

which uses the initialization condition
40\|{\mathbf{M}}_{l}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 40\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{32}\cdot\frac{\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}{L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}^{2}}\sigma_{r}
. It finishes the proof.

Appendix D
Proofs of Low-rank Linear Regression

D.1
Proof of Theorem
2

The following lemma establishes the two-phase regularity conditions of the absolute loss under Gaussian noise.

Lemma 4
.

Assume
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}N(0,\sigma^{2})
and
\{{\rm vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfy Assumption
1
. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{1}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}rd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{1}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}\geq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{2}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:C_{2}\sigma\left(\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{rd_{1}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma\right\},

then with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{1}rd_{1})-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n)
, the absolute loss
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|
satisfies Condition
1
:

1.

the rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sharpness
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
,

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{\ast})\geq\begin{cases}\frac{n}{12}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
\frac{n}{12\sigma}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}^{2},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2};\end{cases}

2.

the rank-r restricted
two-phase sub-gradient bound
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm F,r}\leq\begin{cases}2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
C_{3}n\sigma^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2},\end{cases}

where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is any sub-gradient.

By putting together Lemma
4
and Proposition
1
, we immediately obtain the convergence and statistical performance of RsGrad Algorithm
2
. The proof is a combination of Proposition
1
and Lemma
4
, and is hence omitted.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

We shall proceed assuming the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}=\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{2r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}))|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\}
holds. Specifically, Theorem
3
proves

{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}})\geq 1-\exp(-cd_{1}r/4)-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n).

First consider upper bound of partial Frobenius norm for the sub-gradient
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}
in phase one, where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
and
{\mathbf{M}}
has rank at most
r
. According to event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, it has

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|-{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|+C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

Then by triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}|\langle\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|+C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

Moreover, Corollary
2
proves
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}|\langle\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}
. Thus, together with sample size condition
n\geq c\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}d_{1}r\geq cd_{1}r
, we obtain

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq 2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

Then, recall definition of partial Frobenius norm
\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}
; see Lemma
12
, and it leads to

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq 2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

(13)

On the other hand, by definition of sub-gradient, we have

f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{G}}\rangle=\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2},

and together with Equation (
13
), we have

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq 2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},

which implies
L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}
.

Next consider the lower bound for
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
. We discuss the two phases respectively, namely, phase one when
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma
and phase two when
C\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\sigma\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}<\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma
.

Case 1:

When
\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma=\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}
, under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*})\right]-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq n\sqrt{2/\pi}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}}+\sigma}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq n\sqrt{2/\pi}\frac{\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}}{\sqrt{2}+1}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{12}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where the second line uses
{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
, see Lemma
16
, the penultimate line is from
\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma
and the last line uses
n\geq Cd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
for some absolute constant
C>0
. It shows with
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma
,
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=\frac{1}{12}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}
.

Case 2:

When
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=C\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\sigma\leq\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sigma=\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}
, under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\begin{split}f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*})&\geq\mathbb{E}\left[f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*})\right]-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\geq n\sqrt{2/\pi}\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}}{\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}}+\sigma}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{6}\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny l}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{12}\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny l}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\end{split}

where the penultimate line uses
\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<\sigma
and the last line is from
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq C\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\sigma
. It shows
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=\frac{1}{12}\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny l}}
under
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=C\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\sigma
.

Finally, the following Lemma
5
proves under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
,
L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=Cn\sigma^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}
and thus proof of Lemma
4
completes.
∎

Lemma 5

(Upper bound for sub-gradient)
.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
satisfy
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}:=\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}\sigma
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
be any sub-gradient. Under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq Cn\sigma^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Take
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
, where
\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
is the best rank
r
approximation of
{\mathbf{G}}
. So
\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}_{1})\leq r
. Then by Lemma
16
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})\leq

\displaystyle n\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}-n\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2/\pi}n\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2/\pi}n\frac{C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2C_{{\tiny l}}\langle{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle+(C_{{\tiny u}}-C_{{\tiny l}})\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\left(\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\right),

Besides, event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
shows

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+(2+C_{1})\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

where the second inequality is from the condition
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{nC_{{\tiny u}}}}\sigma
. Then insert
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
into the equation and then we obtain

\begin{split}f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))&-f({\mathbf{M}})\\
&\leq\frac{\sigma}{4nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}^{2}+(1+C_{1}/2)\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}+\frac{n}{\sigma}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(14)

On the other hand, by the definition of sub-gradient, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle{\mathbf{G}},\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle=\frac{\sigma}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

(15)

Combine Equation (
14
) with Equation (
15
) and by solving the quadratic inequality we get

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq Cn\sigma^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
3

The following lemma establishes the two-phase regularity conditions of the absolute loss under heavy-tailed noise.

Lemma 6
.

Assume
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{{\rm vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfy Assumptions
2
and
1
, respectively. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{1}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}rd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{1}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{2}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:C_{2}b_{0}\left(\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{rd_{1}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}<8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

then with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{1}rd_{1})-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n)
, the absolute loss
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|
satisfies Condition
1
:

1.

the rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sharpness
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{\ast})\geq\begin{cases}\frac{n}{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}^{2},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2};\end{cases}

2.

the rank-r restricted
two-phase sub-gradient bound
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm F,r}\leq\begin{cases}2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
C_{3}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2},\end{cases}

where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is any sub-gradient.

The proof of Theorem
3
is a combination of Proposition
1
and Lemma
6
. Thus we only prove Lemma
6
here.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

Suppose the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}=\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{2r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}))|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\}
holds. Specifically, Theorem
3
proves

{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}})\geq 1-\exp(-cd_{1}r/4)-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n).

The
L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}
proof is the same as the one in the proof of Lemma
4
. Then consider lower bound of
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
.

Case 1:
When
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma=:\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}
, by event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left|\langle{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle-\xi_{i}\right|-\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right)-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle\right|-2\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi_{i}|-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq n\sqrt{\frac{2C_{{\tiny l}}}{\pi}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}-2n\gamma-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

where the third line is from triangular inequality and the last line is from Corollary
2
. Then combine with
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
and
n\geq cd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
, and
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
could be further bounded with

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\frac{n}{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

which verifies
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=\frac{n}{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}
under
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
.

Case 2:
Then consider the second phase when
C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}b_{0}=\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}<\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. To bound expectation of
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
, we first consider the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}\left[f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]
. Notice for all
t_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi-t_{0}|&=\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+\int_{s<t_{0}}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)\\
&=2\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)\\
&=2\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+t_{0}-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s),\end{split}

(16)

where the last line is due to

\displaystyle\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)

\displaystyle=-\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,d(1-H_{\xi}(s))

\displaystyle=-(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))\bigg|_{t_{0}}^{+\infty}+\int_{s\geq t_{0}}\left(1-H_{\xi}(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))+\int_{s\geq t_{0}}\left(1-H_{\xi}(s)\right)\,ds,

whose first term vanishes

\displaystyle 0\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})(1-H_{\xi}(s))\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(\xi>s)\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}(s-t_{0})\cdot\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}}{s^{1+\varepsilon}}=0.

Set
t_{0}=\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle
and then Equation (
16
) becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\big|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]=2\int_{s\geq\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}sdH_{\xi}(s).

In the meantime, with
t_{0}=0
, Equation (
16
) becomes

{\mathbb{E}}\left|\xi\right|=2\int_{s\geq 0}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s).

Take the difference of the above two equations and it yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\big|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left|\xi\right|=2\int_{0}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}H_{\xi}(s)ds-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle.

(17)

Note that
\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}))
and denote
z_{i}:=\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle
,
\sigma_{i}^{2}:=\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})
. Note that Assumption
1
, conditions of sensing matrices, implies

\displaystyle C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{i}^{2}\leq C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

Denote the density of
z_{i}
as
f_{z_{i}}(\cdot)
. Take expectation of
z_{i}
on both sides of Equation (
17
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\left(H_{\xi}(s)-0.5\right)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dw\,ds\,dt.

Notice that by conditions on sensing matrices Assumption
1
, we have
\sigma_{i}\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. Hence, when
w\in(-\sigma_{i},\sigma_{i})
, by Assumption
2
, we have
h_{\xi}(w)\geq b_{0}^{-1}
. Thus the above equation could be further bounded with

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}\int_{0}^{t}f_{z_{i}}(t)b_{0}^{-1}s\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=b_{0}^{-1}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}t^{2}f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dt=b_{0}^{-1}\sigma_{i}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-t^{2}/2}\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{6b_{0}}\sigma_{i}^{2},

where the last line is from
\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-t^{2}/2}\,dt\geq 1/6
. Therefore, sum over
i=1.\dots,n
and then it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]\geq\frac{1}{6b_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq\frac{n}{6b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Invoke event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
,

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}[f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})]-C\sqrt{nd_{1}r}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{6b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-C\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

where the last inequality uses
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}
. This proves
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}
.

Finally, from the following lemma, we see that
L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\leq C_{3}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}
when
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
holds.
And this finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7

(Upper bound for sub-gradient)
.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
satisfy
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}:=C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{1}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
be any sub-gradient. Under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq C_{2}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

where
C_{1},C_{2}>0
are absolute constants.

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Take
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
, where
\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
is the best rank
r
approximation of
{\mathbf{G}}
. We finish the proof via combining lower bound and upper bound of
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
.

First consider
{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})
.
Use the same notation as the above proof and similarly,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle}\int_{0}^{\xi}h_{\xi}(x)\,dx\,d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2b_{1}^{-1}\left|\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle}\xi\,d\xi\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b_{1}^{-1}\left|(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)^{2}-(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)^{2}\right|,

where the inequality follows from the upper bound for
h_{\xi}(x)
. Then take expectation with respect to
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on each side and sum up over
i
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-\left|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle b_{1}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left|(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)^{2}-(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq
