\displaystyle nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\left[\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right],

where the last inequality uses Holder’s inequality. Then, bound
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+(2+C_{1})nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where the second inequality is from the condition
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}^{-1}}b_{1}
. Insert
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
and it becomes

\begin{split}f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))&-f({\mathbf{M}})\\
&\leq\frac{b_{1}}{4nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}^{2}+(1+C_{1}/2)\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}.\end{split}

(18)

On the other hand, by the definition of sub-gradient,
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
has lower bound

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{G}}\rangle=\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

(19)

Combine Equation (
18
) with Equation (
19
) and then solve the quadratic inequality which leads to

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq C_{2}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4

The following lemma establishes the two-phase regularity conditions of Huber loss under heavy-tailed noise.

Lemma 8
.

Assume
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{{\rm vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfy Assumptions
3
and
1
, respectively. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{1}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}rd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
and define
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{2}
to be

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{1}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma+2\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\delta\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{2}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:C_{2}b_{0}\left(\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{rd_{1}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}<8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma+2\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\delta\right\},

then with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{1}rd_{1})-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n)
, the Huber loss
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{H,\delta}(Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle)
satisfies Condition
1
:

1.

the rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sharpness
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{\ast})\geq\begin{cases}\frac{\delta n}{2}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
\frac{\delta n}{3b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}^{2},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2};\end{cases}

2.

the rank-r restricted
two-phase sub-gradient bound
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm F,r}\leq\begin{cases}4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
C_{3}\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2},\end{cases}

where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is any sub-gradient.

The proof of Theorem
4
is a combination of Proposition
1
and Lemma
8
. Thus we only prove Lemma
8
here.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

We shall proceed assuming the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}=\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{2r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}))|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\leq C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\}
holds. Specifically, Theorem
3
proves

{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}})\geq 1-\exp(-cd_{1}r/4)-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n).

First consider upper bound of sub-gradient
{\mathbf{G}}\in f({\mathbf{M}})
, where matrix
{\mathbf{M}}
has rank at most
r
. By event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
and Lipschitz
2\delta
continuity of
\rho_{H,\delta}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle)-\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle)-\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)+C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\delta|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle|+C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

Note that Corollary
2
bounds
\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle\right|\leq n\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}=n\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}
and
n\geq cd_{1}r
. Thus the above equation could be upper bounded with

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq 4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}=4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}

On the other hand, by definition of sub-gradient, it has

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}),{\mathbf{G}}\rangle=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

Thus the above two equations lead to

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}\leq f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq 4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

which implies
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq 4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}
, namely,
L_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=4\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}
.

Then consider the lower bound of
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
.

Case 1:
When
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\gamma\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}+2\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}
, use the inequality
2\delta|x|-\delta^{2}\leq\rho_{H,\delta}(x)\leq 2\delta|x|
. Under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})-C_{2}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq 2\delta{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(|\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle|-|\xi_{i}|\right)-n\delta^{2}-C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq 2\delta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|-4\delta\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi_{i}|-n\delta^{2}-C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq 2n\delta\sqrt{\frac{2C_{{\tiny l}}}{\pi}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}-4\delta n\gamma-n\delta^{2}-C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

where the last line is from Corollary
2
and
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi_{i}|=\gamma
. Then with
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\gamma\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}+2\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}
and sample size
n\geq cd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
, it has

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}n\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

It shows with
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=8\gamma\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}+2\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}
, we have
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=\delta n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}/2
.

Case 2:
When
C_{2}b_{0}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 8\gamma\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}+2\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}
,
first consider expectation conditioned on
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
. For any
z\in{\mathbb{R}}
, it has

\begin{split}&{~~~~~}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-z)\right]\\
&=\int_{\delta+z}^{+\infty}2\delta(x-z)-\delta^{2}\,dH_{\xi}(x)+\int_{-\infty}^{z-\delta}2\delta(z-x)-\delta^{2}dH_{\xi}(x)+\int_{z-\delta}^{z+\delta}\left(z-x\right)^{2}dH_{\xi}(x).\end{split}

(20)

Take derivative over variable
z
,

\displaystyle{~~~~~}\frac{d}{d\,z}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-z)\right]

\displaystyle=-\delta^{2}h_{\xi}(\delta+z)-2\delta\int_{\delta+z}^{+\infty}\,dH_{\xi}(x)+\delta^{2}h_{\xi}(z-\delta)+2\delta\int_{-\infty}^{z-\delta}\,dH_{\xi}(x)

\displaystyle{~~~~~}+\delta^{2}h_{\xi}(z+\delta)-\delta^{2}h_{\xi}(z-\delta)-2\int_{z-\delta}^{z+\delta}(x-z)\,dH_{\xi}(x)

\displaystyle=-2\delta+2\delta H_{\xi}(z+\delta)+2\delta H_{\xi}(z-\delta)-2(x-z)H_{\xi}(x)\bigg|_{z-\delta}^{z+\delta}+2\int_{z-\delta}^{z+\delta}H_{\xi_{i}}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=2\int_{z-\delta}^{z+\delta}H_{\xi}(x)\,dx-2\delta,

and take second order derivative,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dz^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-z)\right]

\displaystyle=2(H_{\xi}(z+\delta)-H_{\xi}(z-\delta)).

Assumption
3
guarantees
\frac{d}{d\,z}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-z)\right]\bigg|_{z=0}=0
. Therefore the Taylor expansion at
0
is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-z)\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})\right]+2\int_{0}^{z}(H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta))y\,dy.

Insert
z=\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}))
into the above equation,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]=2\int_{0}^{z}(H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta))y\,dy.

Denote density of
\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle
by
f_{z_{i}}(\cdot)
and denote its variance by
\sigma_{i}^{2}:=\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})
. Take expectation w.r.t.
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
and we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}(H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta))yf_{z_{i}}(t)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}\int_{0}^{t}f_{z_{i}}(t)(H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta))y\,dy\,dt.

Notice that the conditions on sensing matrices Assumption
1
and phase-two region imply
\sigma_{i}\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 8\gamma\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}+2\delta\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}
. Thus for
y\in(-\sigma_{i},\sigma_{i})
, by Assumption
3
, it has
H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta)\geq 2\delta b_{0}^{-1}
. Hence, the above equation could be lower bounded with

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\geq 4\delta b_{0}^{-1}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}t^{2}f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle=4\delta b_{0}^{-1}\sigma_{i}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-t^{2}/2)\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{3}\delta b_{0}^{-1}\sigma_{i}^{2},

where the last line uses
\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-t^{2}/2)\,dt\geq\frac{1}{6}
.
Conditions on sensing matrices Assumption
1
also imply
\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
. Therefore,
{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
has the following lower bound

{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i})\right]\geq\frac{2}{3}n\delta b_{0}^{-1}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Based on event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}[f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})]-C_{2}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Thus when
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq b_{0}C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}
, we have

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\frac{1}{3}\delta nb_{0}^{-1}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

It proves under
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=b_{0}C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}
, it has
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=\frac{1}{3}\delta nb_{0}^{-1}C_{{\tiny l}}
. The following lemma shows when
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
holds, it has
L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=C_{2}\delta C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
and the the proof completes.
∎

Lemma 9

(Upper bound for sub-gradient)
.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
satisfy
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}:=c\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
be the sub-gradient. Under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq C_{2}\delta\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
.

Proof.

Take
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny l}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
. First consider
{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})
. Continue from Equation (
20
),

\displaystyle~~~~{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{H,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}[H_{\xi}(y+\delta)-H_{\xi}(y-\delta)]y\,dy

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta b_{1}^{-1}\left|\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}y\,dy\right|

\displaystyle=2\delta b_{1}^{-1}\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle^{2}+2\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|.

Then take expectation over
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on each side and sum up over
i
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}[\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}],

which uses assumptions of sensing operators. Then invoke event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
,
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
could be bounded with:

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+4\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+C_{1}\delta\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+(C_{1}+4)\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

where the last inequality uses
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=c\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}\geq c\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{C_{{\tiny u}}}}b_{1}
. Insert
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
and then it is

\begin{split}f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq\frac{b_{1}}{8n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}+\frac{C_{1}+4}{4}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.\end{split}

(21)

On the other hand, from the definition of the sub-gradient, we have

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle{\mathbf{G}},\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle=\frac{b_{1}}{4n\delta C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

(22)

Combine Equation (
21
) with Equation (
22
) and then the quadratic inequality of
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}
gives

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq C_{2}\delta nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
5

The two-phase regularity of the quantile loss is provided by the following lemma.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose Assumption
4
holds. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{1}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}rd_{1}\max\{\delta^{2},(1-\delta)^{2}\}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
abd
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{1}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{2}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}b_{0}\Big(\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{rd_{1}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}<8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

then with probability over
1-\exp(-c_{1}rd_{1})-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n)
, the quantile loss
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{Q,\delta}(Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle)
satisfies Condition
1
:

1.

rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sharpness
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
,

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{\ast})\geq\begin{cases}\frac{n}{8}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
\frac{n}{24b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}^{2},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2};\end{cases}

2.

rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sub-gradient bound
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm F,r}\leq\begin{cases}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
C_{3}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2},\end{cases}

where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is any sub-gradient.

By combining Proposition
1
with Lemma
10
, we get the convergence dynamics and statistical accuracy of RsGrad Algorithm
2
for quantile loss, namely, Theorem
5
.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Notice that quantile loss function
\rho_{Q,\delta}(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous with
\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}
and satisfies
f(x_{1}+x_{2})\leq f(x_{1})+f(x_{2})
.

Suppose the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}=\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{2r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}))|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\leq C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\}
holds. Specifically, Theorem
3
proves

{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}})\geq 1-\exp(-cd_{1}r/4)-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n).

First consider upper bound of sub-gradient
{\mathbf{G}}\in f({\mathbf{M}})
, where matrix
{\mathbf{M}}
has rank at most
r
. By event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)\right]+C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\rho_{Q,\delta}(-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle)+C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}+C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}

\displaystyle\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

where the last line uses
n\geq cd_{1}r\cdot\max\{\delta^{2},(1-\delta)^{2}\}
. On the other hand, by definition of sub-gradient, we have

f({\mathbf{M}}+\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\langle{\mathbf{G}},\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})\rangle=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

Then combine the above two equations and it leads to

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}.

Then consider the lower bound of
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
.

Case 1:
When
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. Notice that quantile loss function satisfies triangle inequality,
\rho_{Q,\delta}(x_{1}+x_{2})\leq\rho_{Q,\delta}(x_{1})+\rho_{Q,\delta}(x_{2})
with which we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi_{i})\right]-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\rho_{Q,\delta}(-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi_{i})+\rho_{Q,\delta}(-\xi_{i})\right]-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}-n\gamma-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Thus under the condition
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
and
n\geq Cd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\cdot\max\{\delta^{2},(1-\delta)^{2}\}
, it has

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})\geq\frac{n}{8}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

which verifies with
\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
,
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny comp}}=\frac{1}{8}n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}
.

Case 2:
Then consider the second phase where
c\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}}b_{0}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. Notice for all fixed
t_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-t_{0})

\displaystyle=\delta\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+(1-\delta)\int_{s<t_{0}}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(s-t_{0})\,dH_{\xi}(s)+(1-\delta)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(t_{0}-s)\,dH_{\xi}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{s\geq t_{0}}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+(1-\delta)t_{0}-(1-\delta)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s),

where the last line is same as the absolute loss case. Set
t_{0}=\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle
and it becomes

\displaystyle{~~~~~}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{s\geq\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds+(1-\delta)\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle-(1-\delta)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s).

With
t_{0}=0
, it becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]=\int_{s\geq 0}(1-H_{\xi}(s))\,ds-(1-\delta)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}s\,dH_{\xi}(s).

The above two equations lead to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]=\int_{0}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}H_{\xi}(s)\,ds-\delta\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle.

(23)

Note that
\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}))
. Denote
z_{i}:=\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle
and
\sigma_{i}^{2}:=\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})
. Let
f_{z_{i}}(\cdot)
be the density of
z_{i}
. Take expectation of
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on both sides of Equation (
23
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\left(H_{\xi}(s)-\delta\right)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}h_{\xi}(w)f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dw\,ds\,dt

Notice that sensing matrices conditions imply
\sigma_{i}\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
. Then when
w\in(-\sigma_{i},\sigma_{i})
, under Assumption
4
, it has
h_{\xi}(w)\geq b_{0}^{-1}
. Thus it has the following upper bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi)\right]

\displaystyle\geq b_{0}^{-1}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}\int_{0}^{t}f_{z_{i}}(t)s\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}b_{0}^{-1}\int_{-\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i}}t^{2}f_{z_{i}}(t)\,dt=\frac{1}{2}b_{0}^{-1}\sigma_{i}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-t^{2}/2}\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{12b_{0}}\sigma_{i}^{2},

and the last line is from
\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}e^{-t^{2}/2}dt\geq 1/6
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi)\right]\geq\frac{1}{12b_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Invoke event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
,

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}-C_{2}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{24b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

where the last inequality uses
\|{\mathbf{X}}-{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq c\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}
. This proves the lower bound in the second phase and shows
\mu_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}=\frac{n}{24b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}
.

Finally, from the following lemma, we see that under
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
,
L_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}\leq C_{2}nb_{1}^{-1}
. And this finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 11

(Upper bound for sub-gradient)
.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
satisfy
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq\tau_{\textsf{\tiny stat}}:=c\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{1}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
be the sub-gradient. Under the event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
, we have
\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq Cnb_{1}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Proof.

Take
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2n}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
, where
\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
is the best rank
r
approximation of
{\mathbf{G}}
and then
\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}_{1})\leq 2r
. First consider
{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})
.
Use the same notation as in the above proof and similarly,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\bigg|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle}\int_{0}^{\xi}h_{\xi}(x)\,dx\,d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle b_{1}^{-1}\left|\int_{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle}^{\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle}\xi\,d\xi\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2b_{1}}\left|(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)^{2}-(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)^{2}\right|,

where the inequality follows from the upper bound for
h_{\xi}(x)\leq b_{1}^{-1}
. Then take expectation over
{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
on each side and sum up over
i=1,\dots,n
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)-\rho_{Q,\delta}(\xi-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2b_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left|(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)^{2}-(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2b_{1}}C_{{\tiny u}}\left|\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right|.

Bound
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}
:

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{M}})+C_{1}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2b_{1}}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+(0.5+C_{1})nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{1}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where the second inequality is from the condition
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\geq c\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{1}\geq c\max\{\delta,1-\delta\}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{C_{{\tiny l}}}}b_{1}
. Since
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}={\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}})
, it becomes

\begin{split}&f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\\
&{~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~}\leq\frac{b_{1}}{8nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}^{2}+(1/2+C_{1})\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}.\end{split}

(24)

On the other hand, by the definition of sub-gradient,
f({\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{M}})
has lower bound

\displaystyle f({\mathbf{M}}+\frac{b_{1}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\text{SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{G}}))-f({\mathbf{M}})\geq\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{2nC_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}.

(25)

Combine Equation (
24
), Equation (
25
) and then solve the quadratic inequality which leads to

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\leq Cnb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

∎

Appendix E
Proof of Initializations

Proof of Theorem
6
.

To start with, consider

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~}=\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\xi_{i}+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right)\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~}\leq\underbrace{\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|}_{B_{1}}+\underbrace{\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\|}_{B_{2}}.

Analysis of
B_{1}

First show
\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))
is an unbiased estimator of
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
. Or equivalently,
\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})
is an unbiased estimator of
\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})^{\top}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})^{\top}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*}),

where the second line switches vector and scalar. Then we shall use Bernstein Theorem
10
to bound
B_{1}
. Note that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})),\quad{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\sim N(0,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}),

(26)

and it implies

\displaystyle\left\|\|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\|_{\Psi_{2}}\left\|\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\sqrt{\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}^{*})}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|

\displaystyle\leq c\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Besides, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}\|{\mathbb{E}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|{\mathbb{E}}\|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|+{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{2}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{2}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{2}.

Note that
\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|=\sigma_{1}
and by
ab\leq\frac{a^{2}}{2c}+\frac{cb^{2}}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~~}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{2}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d_{1}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{4}+\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}{2d_{1}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{4}

Equation (
26
) suggests
{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{4}\leq 3C_{{\tiny u}}^{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{4}
and
{\mathbb{E}}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{4}\leq cC_{{\tiny l}}^{-2}d_{1}^{2}
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle^{2}\|\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\|^{2}\leq\tilde{c}d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}\|{\mathbb{E}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\tilde{c}d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

and it verifies

\displaystyle{~~~~~}\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\right)/n

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\tilde{c}d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq cd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle{~~~~~}\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\right)/n

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\tilde{c}d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq cd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

Thus, Bernstein Theorem
10
implies the following equation

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\sqrt{d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d_{1}}{n}}+c_{1}\frac{\log d_{1}}{n}\log\left(\frac{c\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}}{\sqrt{cd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}

holds with probability over
1-c_{1}d_{1}^{-1}
.

Analysis of
B_{2}

Notice that given
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
with probability exceding
1-c_{2}\exp(-d_{1})
, we have,

\displaystyle B_{2}\leq c_{3}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Then by Lemma
17
, we have with probability exceeding
1-c_{3}(\log d_{1})^{-1}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\leq(2n\gamma_{1}\log d_{1})^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon}}.

Thus, we have the upper bound for
B_{2}
,

\displaystyle B_{2}\leq c_{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}}{n^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}}(\log d_{1})^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}.

Combine
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
and then we obtain

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}+c_{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}}{n^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}}(\log d_{1})^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}

holds with probability exceeding
1-c_{1}d_{1}^{-1}-c_{2}\exp(-d_{1})-c_{3}(\log d_{1})^{-1}
. Then with perturbation theories Lemma
14
and sample size condition

n\geq C\max\left\{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\kappa^{2}d_{1}r^{2}\log d_{1},\left(C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}d_{1}r\right)^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon}}\cdot\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\sigma_{r}^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}}(\log d_{1})^{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\right\},

we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle=\|\text{SVD}_{r}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\text{mat}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}))\right)-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}_{0}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}+\tilde{c}_{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}}{n^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}}(\log d_{1})^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}},

which implies

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2r}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\leq c\sigma_{r},

and it completes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Technical Lemma

The following norm relations are used as facts,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{B}}\|,\quad\left|\langle{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}}\rangle\right|\leq\|{\mathbf{C}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm{F}},

where
{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}}
are matrices with appropriate dimensions.

Lemma 12

(Partial Frobenius Norm, Lemma 15 of
Tong et al. 2021a
)
.

Define partial Frobenius norm of matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
to be

\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}:=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}^{2}({\mathbf{M}})}=\|{\rm SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{M}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

For any
{\mathbf{M}},\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
with
\operatorname{rank}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})\leq r
, one has

\displaystyle|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\rangle|\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}.

For any
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
,
{\mathbf{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}
, one has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{V}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|{\mathbf{V}}\|.

Note that in proofs of main theorems, we actually need to bound
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}
rather than the term
\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}
. While,
\|{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\|_{\rm{F},r}
provides a satisfactory upper bound for
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}_{l}}({\mathbf{G}}_{l})\|_{\rm{F}}
, see the followed lemma.

Lemma 13
.

(Frobenius norm of projected sub-gradient)
Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
be a matrix with rank at most
r
. Suppose
\mathbf{G}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is the sub-gradient at
{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathbb{T}
is the tangent space for
{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
at
{\mathbf{M}}
. Then we have

\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{G})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{U}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
be SVD of
{\mathbf{M}}
with orthogonal matrices
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times r}
,
{\mathbf{V}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times r}
. Notice that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}){\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{T}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

and
\text{rank}({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})=\text{rank}({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})=r
. Then by properties of partial Frobenius norm Lemma
12
, one has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}=\|{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|{\mathbf{U}}\|=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}){\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{T}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

which implies
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq 2\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}
.
∎

The following lemma is critical in establishing convergence of Riemannian optimizations.

Lemma 14
.

(Matrix Perturbation)
Suppose matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
has rank
r
and has singular value decomposition
{\mathbf{M}}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\operatorname{diag}\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\cdots,\sigma_{r}\}
and
\sigma_{1}\geq\sigma_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{r}>0
. Then for any
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfying
\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<\sigma_{r}/8
, with
\hat{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times r}
the left and right singular vectors of
r
largest singular values, we have

\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|,\quad\|\hat{\mathbf{V}}\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{8}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|,

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}}\|+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{r}},

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}}.

Proof.

See Section
G
.
∎

Lemma 15
.

Suppose random matrix
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
follows mean zero multivariate Gaussian distribution
N(\boldsymbol{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
where
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}d_{2}\times d_{1}d_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\leq C_{{\tiny u}}.

Then its operator norm could be upper bounded by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\geq t+c\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}(d_{1}+d_{2})}\right)\leq\exp(-t^{2}),

where
c>0
is some constant. It implies
\left\|\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq c_{1}\sqrt{d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}}
and
\left\|\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{d_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}}
, with some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

Proof.

The proof follows
\varepsilon
-net arguments. Corollary 4.2.13 of work
Vershynin 2018
shows that take
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{4}
and then there exists an
\varepsilon
-net
{\mathcal{N}}
of sphere
{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{1}-1}
and an
\varepsilon
-net
{\mathcal{M}}
of sphere
{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{2}-1}
with cardinalities

|{\mathcal{N}}|\leq 9^{d_{1}},\quad|{\mathcal{M}}|\leq 9^{d_{2}}.

For any fixed unit vector
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, consider
{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}

\displaystyle=\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}})^{\top}({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}})\sim N(0,({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}})),

where
\otimes
is Kronecker product. Besides, it has
\|{\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}=1
and
({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{v}})\leq C_{{\tiny u}}
. Thus we have, for any
t>1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}}\right).

Take the union over
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
and
{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
and then it leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}},\,{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}\geq t\right)\leq 9^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}}\right).

On the other hand, by Theorem 4.4.5 of
Vershynin 2018
, we have
\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\leq 2\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}},\,{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}
, by which we obtain,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\geq 2t\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}},\,{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}\geq t\right)\leq 9^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2C_{{\tiny u}}}\right).

It implies
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\mathbf{X}}\|\geq t+c\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}(d_{1}+d_{2})}\right)\leq\exp(-t^{2})
and the proof completes.
∎

Lemma 16
.

(Expectation of absolute loss under Gaussian noise) Suppose the noise term follows Gaussian distribution
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}N(0,\sigma^{2})
and Gaussian sensing operator vectorization
\text{vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})
satisfy Assumption
1
. Take the absolute value loss function

f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|,

and then for any matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{M}^{*})]=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma,

\displaystyle n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{M})]\leq n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny u}}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}.

Proof.

Note that
f(\mathbf{M})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|
,
f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|
.

First calculate the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|\big\lvert\mathbf{X}_{i}]
. Notice that conditioned on
\mathbf{X}_{i}
,
\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle
is normally distributed with mean
-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle
and variance
\sigma^{2}
. Then
|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|
has a folded normal distribution.

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\left[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|\big|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right]\\
&=\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\exp(-\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}})-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle[1-2\phi(\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma})],\end{split}

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the normal cumulative distribution function. For each
i=1,2,\cdots,n
, one has

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|&=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|\big\lvert\mathbf{X}_{i}]\right]\\
&=\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\right]\\
&{~~~~~~~~~~~~~}-\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle\cdot\left[1-2\phi(\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma})\right]\right].\end{split}

Notice that
\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}))
and then after integration calculation it is

\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})}.

Specifically, when
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{M}}^{*}
, it leads to
\mathbb{E}|\xi|=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma
.
Thus, sum over
i=1,\dots,n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{\mathbf{M}}^{*})

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}|\xi|=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})}.

Then by conditions of sensing operators Assumption
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M})\leq n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}.

∎

Corollary 2
.

Under same conditions of Lemma
16
, we have

\displaystyle n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\langle{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Proof.

Take noiseless case in Lemma
16
and it yields
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\langle{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle\right|
.
∎

Proposition 3
.

(Empirical process in matrix recovery)
Suppose
f(\mathbf{M})
is defined in Equation (
4
) with sensing matrices in Assumption
1
and
\rho(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous with
\tilde{L}
. Then there exists constants
C,C_{1}>0
, such that, with probability over
1-\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}d_{1}r}{3}\right)-3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\log n}\right)
,

\big|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-(\mathbb{E}f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbb{E}f({\mathbf{M}}))\big|\leq C\tilde{L}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

(27)

holds for all
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
and
\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}
.

Proof.

See Section
H
.
∎

Lemma 17
.

(Tail inequality for sum of squared heavy-tailed random variables)
Suppose
\xi,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}
are i.i.d. random variables and there exists
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
such that its
1+\varepsilon
moment is finite, that is
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}<+\infty
. Denote
\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}=\left({\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}\right)^{1/(1+\varepsilon)}
. Then for any
s>0
, tail bound for sum of squares could be upper bounded

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}>s\right)\leq 2n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Proof.

For any
s>0
, it has
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{2}\geq s\right)\leq s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}
which uses Markov’s inequality. Introduce truncated random variable
\varphi_{i}:=\xi_{i}^{2}1_{\{\xi_{i}^{2}<s\}}
. Note that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}
could be written into sum of two parts

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}^{2}-\varphi_{i}).

Hence, we analyze these two parts respectively. First consider probability of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{2}>s\right)\leq n\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}}{s^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}}=n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Then bound tail for sum of
\varphi_{i}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq ns^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\varphi=ns^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\varphi^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}\varphi^{\frac{1-\varepsilon}{2}}\leq n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Finally, combine the above two equations, it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}>s\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)\leq 2n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 10
.

(Bernstein’s Inequality, Proposition 2 in
Koltchinskii et al. 2011
)
Let
{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{A}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbf{A}}_{n}
be independent
p\times q
matrices that satisfy for some
\alpha\geq 1
(and all
i
)

{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}=0,\qquad\|\Lambda_{\max}({\mathbf{A}}_{i})\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}=:K<+\infty.

Define

S^{2}:=\max\left\{\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\big/n,\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right)\big/n\right\}.

Then for some constant
C>0
and for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right)\big/n\geq CS\sqrt{\frac{t+\log(p+q)}{n}}+CK\log^{1/\alpha}\left(\frac{K}{S}\right)\frac{t+\log(p+q)}{n}\right)\leq\exp(-t).

Appendix G
Proof of Matrix Perturbation Lemma
14

To prove Lemma
14
, first consider a simpler case, when the matrix and the perturbation are symmetric. The core of the proof is Theorem 1 of work
Xia 2021
and it is presented here.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}
be symmetric
d\times d
matrices. The matrix
{\mathbf{M}}
has rank
r=\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}})\leq d
with eigen-decomposition
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{U}}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times r}
contains orthonormal columns and
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}=\text{diag}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{r})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r\times r}
is diagonal.

Let
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(d-r)}
be complement of
{\mathbf{U}}
such that
[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
is orthonormal. Suppose
\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times r}
contains eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathbf{Z}}
corresponding to largest
r
eigenvalues in absolute values.

Define the following projectors

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{P}}^{0}:={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top},\quad{\mathfrak{P}}^{-k}:={\mathbf{U}}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{-k}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},\quad\text{ for all }k\geq 1,

(28)

and denote the
k
-th order perturbation term

\displaystyle S_{{\mathbf{M}},k}({\mathbf{Z}}):=\sum_{{\mathbf{s}}:s_{1}+\dots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau({\mathbf{s}})}\cdot{\mathfrak{P}}^{-s_{1}}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathfrak{P}}^{-s_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\cdots{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathfrak{P}}^{-s_{k+1}},

(29)

where
{\mathbf{s}}=(s_{1},\dots,s_{k+1})
contains non-negative integer indices and
\tau({\mathbf{s}}):=\sum_{j=1}^{k+1}1_{s_{j}>0}
.

Theorem 11

(Theorem 1 of
Xia 2021
)
.

If
\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|<\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq r}|\lambda_{i}|}{2}
, then it has

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}=\sum_{k\geq 1}S_{{\mathbf{M}},k}({\mathbf{Z}}).

Lemma 18
.

(Symmetric Matrix Perturbation)
Suppose symmetric matrix
\mathbf{M}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
has rank
r
. Let
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
be its singular value decomposition with
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}=\operatorname{diag}\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{r}\}
,
|\lambda_{1}|\geq|\lambda_{2}|\geq\cdots\geq|\lambda_{r}|>0
. Then for any matrix
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
satisfying
\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<|\lambda_{r}|/8
with
\hat{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
eigenvectors of
r
largest absolute eigenvalues of
\hat{\mathbf{M}}
, then we have

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{8\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|},

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\|\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|+40\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|},

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+40\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Proof.

Denote
\mathbf{Z}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}
. Define
\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-r)}
such that
[\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}_{\perp}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is orthonormal. The projectors
{\mathfrak{P}}^{-k}
, for non-negative integers
k
and the
k
-th order perturbation term are given by Equation (
28
) and Equation (
29
) respectively.

Then norm of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})
could be bounded in the way of

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|\leq\binom{2k}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k}}\leq\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k},\quad\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\binom{2k}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k}}\leq\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k}.

Hence, sum up the above equations over
k
and by Theorem
11
, it leads to

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|},\quad\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

(30)

Then consider
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}&=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{M}\\
&=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\mathbf{U}^{\top}.\end{split}

Note that when
s_{k+1}=0
,
\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}
, it has
\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{M}=0
and thus we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k,\,s_{k+1}>0}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}{\mathbf{U}}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k,\,s_{k+1}=1}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}

\displaystyle{~~~}+\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k,\,s_{k+1}\geq 2}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{1-s_{k+1}}

The operator norm of
\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|
could be bounded with

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|\leq\binom{2k-1}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k-1}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1},

which implies

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Specifically, when
k=1
, it is
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},1}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
. Similarly,
\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathbf{M}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}
holds. In parallel, Frobenius norm of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}
has

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\binom{2k-1}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k-1}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k-1}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1},

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|},\quad\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathbf{M}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

(31)

Then expand
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}
,

\begin{split}&{~~~~}\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\\
&=\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{Z})\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}\\
&=\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{Z})\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\\
&=(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{M}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\\
&=\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})+\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\\
&{~~~}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},\end{split}

where the last equation follows from Theorem
11
and
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},1}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
,
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathbf{M}},1}({\mathbf{Z}})={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}
. Then consider the latter three terms of the above equations,

\displaystyle{~~~}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}

\displaystyle{~~~}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}

\displaystyle{~~~}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}),

where the last equation is due to
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}={\mathbf{I}}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}=({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}){\mathbf{Z}}({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top})
. Hence, we get the expansion of
\textrm{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})
,

\displaystyle{~~~~}\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})+\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}

\displaystyle{~~~}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}).

Take Frobenius norm on each side of the above equation and it leads to

\begin{split}&{~~~~}\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\leq\underbrace{\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{1}}+\underbrace{\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{2}}+\underbrace{\|\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{3}}\\
&{~~~}+\underbrace{\|(\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{4}}+\underbrace{\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{5}}.\end{split}

(32)

Note that by Equation (
31
), we have upper bound of
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
,

\displaystyle A_{1}\vee A_{2}\leq\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\vee\|\mathbf{M}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Besides, it has
{\mathbf{Z}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
and
\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\|({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}){\mathbf{Z}}({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
and it leads to

\displaystyle A_{3}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2},

which shows
A_{3}\leq\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\rm{F}}
. With Equation (
30
),
A_{4}
could be upper bounded by

\displaystyle A_{4}\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|\leq\frac{8\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Note that with triangular inequality and in a similar fashion, it has

\displaystyle A_{5}\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})\|_{\rm{F}}+\|(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{16\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Thus, Equation (
32
) could be further upper bounded by

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\rm{F}}+40\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}=\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Likewise, one has the bound of the operator norm

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

The proof completes.
∎

Then we are ready to prove Lemma
14
, the assymetric case.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

To apply Lemma
18
, first construct symmetrization of
\mathbf{M}
and
\hat{\mathbf{M}}
,

\mathbf{Y}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\mathbf{M}\\
\mathbf{M}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(d_{1}+d_{2})\times(d_{1}+d_{2})},\,\hat{\mathbf{Y}}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\hat{\mathbf{M}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(d_{1}+d_{2})\times(d_{1}+d_{2})}.

Denote the perturbation matrix as
\mathbf{Z}:=\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}
and similarly define

\hat{\mathbf{Z}}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\mathbf{Z}\\
\mathbf{Z}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\\
\hat{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right).

Note that
\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{Y})=2\operatorname{rank}({\mathbf{M}})=2r
. First analyze SVD of matrix
{\mathbf{Y}}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
. Notice that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2r}

contains orthonormal columns.
Besides, it has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}&0\\
0&-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\end{matrix}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&{\mathbf{U}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{V}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}0&{\mathbf{M}}\\
{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)={\mathbf{Y}},

by which we obtain SVD of
{\mathbf{Y}}
. Without loss of generality, suppose
d_{1}\geq d_{2}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}=\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}
is full SVD of
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}
, with
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
,
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\text{diag}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\sigma}_{d_{2}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times d_{2}}
,
\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times d_{2}}
, where
\tilde{\sigma}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\tilde{\sigma}_{d_{2}}\geq 0
. Thus, leading
r
components of
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}
is
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})=\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\hat{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}
, where
\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{V}}}
are first
r
columns of full matrix
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}
and
\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}
respectively and
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}=\text{diag}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\sigma}_{r})
.

Notice that since
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
is symmetrization for
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}
, its full SVD is

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}&\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\\
\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}&-\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}&0\\
0&-\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\end{matrix}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}&\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\\
\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}&-\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top},

(33)

which has eigenvalues
\tilde{\sigma}_{1},-\tilde{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\sigma}_{d_{2}},-\tilde{\sigma}_{d_{2}}
. Thus, largest
2r
eigenvalues of
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
in absolute are
\tilde{\sigma}_{1},-\tilde{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\sigma}_{r},-\tilde{\sigma}_{r}
. Thus, since
\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}
,
\hat{{\mathbf{V}}}
are first
r
columns of
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}
,
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}
respectively, Equation (
33
) infers

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Y}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}&0\\
0&-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\end{matrix}\right),

which means
\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}})
is

\displaystyle\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}&0\\
0&-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\end{matrix}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})\\
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right),

and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)
contains leadning
2r
eigenvectors of
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}})-{\mathbf{Y}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}\\
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})^{\top}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right).

It infers
