\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}})-{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{\rm{F}}=\sqrt{2}\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}.

(34)

Then apply Lemma
18
to the rank
2r
symmetric matrix
{\mathbf{Y}}
and the symmetric perturbed matrix
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
,

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{\mathbf{Y}})-\mathbf{Y}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{\rm{F}}+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|}{\sigma_{r}},

(35)

which uses
|\lambda_{2r}({\mathbf{Y}})|=\sigma_{r}
. Note that definition of
{\mathbf{Y}}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
implies
\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{\rm{F}}=\sqrt{2}\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}
and
\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|=\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|
, respectively. Then, Equation (
34
), Equation (
35
) infer,

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|}{\sigma_{r}}.

Notice that proof of operator norm version
\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}\|\leq\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|+40\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{r}}
is similar to the Frobinius norm case and hence it is skipped.

Apply the perturbated eigenvector results of Lemma
18
to the symmetric rank
2r
matrix
{\mathbf{Y}}
and symmetric perturbed matrix
\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}
,

\bigg\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\bigg\|\leq\frac{8\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|}{\sigma_{r}},

which could be simplified as

\displaystyle\|\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}&0\\
0&\hat{{\mathbf{V}}}\hat{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)-\left(\begin{matrix}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}&0\\
0&{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)\|\leq\frac{8\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|}{\sigma_{r}}.

Then by
\|\hat{{\mathbf{Y}}}-{\mathbf{Y}}\|=\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|
and block matrix properties, it arrives at

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{8}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|,\quad\|\hat{\mathbf{V}}\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{8}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|.

∎

Appendix H
Proof of Matrix Recovery Empirical Process Proposition
3

Proof.

The proof follows from Theorem
14
. Recall
{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:=\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}:\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}})\leq r\}
is the set of matrices with rank bounded by
r
. Consider

\displaystyle~~~~Z:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}\left|f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}))\right|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}.

Note that
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle-Y_{i})
and then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}Z

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\big(\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle-Y_{i})-\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle-Y_{i})\big)\right|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\right|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot\sqrt{r}{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|,

where
\{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is Rademacher sequence independent of
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
. The first inequality is from Theorem
12
and the second inequality is from Theorem
13
. Notice that
\text{vec}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)\sim N(\boldsymbol{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i})
and
\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\|\leq nC_{{\tiny u}}
.
Lemma
15
implies
{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{nd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}}
for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
. Thus we have

{\mathbb{E}}Z\leq C_{1}\tilde{L}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}.

For simplicity, denote
f_{i}({\mathbf{M}},\Delta{\mathbf{M}}):=|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{~~~}\|\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}},\Delta{\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f_{i}({\mathbf{M}},\Delta{\mathbf{M}})|\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\log n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})|\|_{\Psi_{1}}+2\log n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})|,

which uses inequality for maximum of sub-exponential variables. Moreover, for each
i=1,\dots,n
, it has

\displaystyle~~~~\|\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}})|\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\|\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|\sup_{\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\tilde{L}\sqrt{r}\|\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}},

for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
and the last line is from definition of partial Frobenius norm Lemma
12
and random multivariate Gaussian matrix norm Lemma
15
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}})|

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}\\
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\end{subarray}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)|\cdot\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,r}}\leq C_{3}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}.

Combine the above three equations and then it has

\displaystyle\|\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|f_{i}({\mathbf{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}f_{i}({\mathbf{M}})|\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C_{4}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\log n.

Also, note that
\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\sim N(0,\text{vec}(\Delta{\mathbf{M}})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}(\Delta{\mathbf{M}}))
and
\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}\text{vec}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})\leq C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{~~~~}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}+\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)\big)^{2}\big]/\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}^{2}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\rangle^{2}/\|\Delta{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\tiny u}}\tilde{L}^{2}.

Invoke Theorem
14
and Remark
2
, and take
\alpha=1
,
\eta=1
,
\delta=0.5
and there exists some constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}nd_{1}r}+t)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{3nC_{{\tiny u}}\tilde{L}^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\frac{t}{C_{5}\tilde{L}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}d_{1}r}\log n}\right)

holds for any
t>0
.
Take
t=C\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}}\tilde{L}
and then we have

\mathbb{P}(Z\geq C\tilde{L}\sqrt{nd_{1}rC_{{\tiny u}}})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}d_{1}r}{3}\right)+3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\log n}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 12

(Symmetrization of Expectations,
[
Van Der Vaart et al. 1996
]
)
.

Consider
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
independent matrices in
\chi
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of real-valued functions on
\chi
. Let
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}
be a Rademacher sequence independent of
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
, then

\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f(\mathbf{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i}))\big|\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\big|\big]

(36)

Theorem 13

(Contraction Theorem,
[
Ludoux and Talagrand 1991
]
)
.

Consider the non-random elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
of
\chi
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of real-valued functions on
\chi
. Consider the Lipschitz continuous functions
\rho_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with Lipschitz constant
L
, i.e.

\left|\rho_{i}(\mu)-\rho_{i}(\tilde{\mu})\right|\leq L|\mu-\tilde{\mu}|,\text{ for all }\mu,\tilde{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}

Let
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}
be a Rademacher sequence
.
Then for any function
f^{*}:\chi\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\left\{\rho_{i}\left(f\left(x_{i}\right)\right)-\rho_{i}\left(f^{*}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\right\}\right|\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[L\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mid\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\left(f\left(x_{i}\right)-f^{*}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\mid\right]

(37)

Theorem 14

(Tail inequality for suprema of empirical process
[
Adamczak 2008
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be independent random variables with values in a measurable space
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{B})
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a countable class of measurable functions
f:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Assume that for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
and every
i,\mathbb{E}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)=0
and for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
and all
i,\left\|\sup_{\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}<\infty.
Let

Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|

Define moreover

\sigma^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}

Then, for all
0<\eta<1
and
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(\alpha,\eta,\delta)
, such that for all
t\geq 0

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(Z&\geq(1+\eta)\mathbb{E}Z+t)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta)\mathbb{E}Z-t)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right)^{\alpha}\right)

Remark 2
.

Notice that here we require
\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})=0
and when
\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\neq 0
,
\mathbf{Z}
should be

Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\right|,

and tail inequality of
Z
would be

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(Z&\geq(1+\eta)\mathbb{E}Z+t)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}f({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\end{split}

Appendix I
Proof of Outlier Case

Proof of Theorem
7

We only need to prove the following two-phase regularity properties, with which we could obtain the convergence dynamics similarly to Section
B
.

Lemma 19
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
holds and
2
holds for inliers. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{0}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})
and
\epsilon\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}}{4\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}+\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}}
, then with probability over
1-2\exp\big(-c_{0}\tilde{s}\log\big(2d/\tilde{s})\big)-3\exp\big(-((1-\epsilon)n\log(2d/\tilde{s}))^{1/2}\log^{-1}(1-\epsilon)n\big)
, we have

1.

for all
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\;\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq 8C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1/2}\gamma,\ |\text{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\leq\tilde{s}\right\}
and for all sub-gradient
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq\frac{n}{5}C_{{\tiny l}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\quad{\rm and}\quad\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq nC_{{\tiny u}}^{1/2},

where
\Omega:=\text{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
and
\Pi:=\text{supp}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{\tilde{s}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega^{\rm c}}({\mathbf{G}}))\big)
;

2.

for all

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\ C_{2}C_{{\tiny u}}^{1/2}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\cdot b_{0}\max\left\{\big(\tilde{s}/n\cdot\log(2d/\tilde{s})\big)^{1/2},\epsilon\right\}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 8C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1/2}\gamma,|\text{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\leq\tilde{s}\right\}

and for all sub-gradient
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq\frac{nC_{{\tiny l}}}{12b_{0}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\quad{\rm and}\quad\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq C_{3}\frac{nC_{{\tiny u}}}{b_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2},

where
\Omega:=\text{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
and
\Pi:=\text{supp}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{\tilde{s}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega^{\rm c}}({\mathbf{G}}))\big)
.

Proof of Lemma
19
.

The difference with Lemma
1
is the appearance of outliers and we shall treat inliers, outliers differently. Denote

\displaystyle f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}):=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\right|,\quad f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}):=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\right|.

Notice that we have
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})=f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})+f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. We shall assume the event

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\;|\text{supp}(\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\leq 3\tilde{s}}\left|f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-{\mathbb{E}}\left[f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right]\right|\cdot\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\leq C\sqrt{(1-\varepsilon)n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\right\}

holds. Proposition
2
proves
{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{i})\geq 1-\exp(-\frac{C^{2}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{3})-3\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)n\log(2d/\tilde{s})}}{\log(1-\epsilon)n}\right)
. Also, we assume the event

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{o}:=\left\{\sup_{|\text{supp}(\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\leq 3\tilde{s}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\bigg||\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle|-{\mathbb{E}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle|\bigg|\cdot\|\Delta{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}^{-1}\leq C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\right\}

holds and epsilon net theory or Gaussian process theory shows
{\mathbb{P}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{o})\geq 1-\exp(-\frac{C^{2}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{3})
.

Phase One

First, show
\mu
-sharpness. Notice that

f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}).

Same as Section
B
, under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}
, we have

f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq(1-\epsilon)n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}\pi/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-C\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-2(1-\epsilon)n\gamma.

At the same time, under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{o}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\right|-\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\rangle\right|+C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}+C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2},

where the last line follows from Lemma
16
. Thus, we have lower bound of
f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})
,

\displaystyle~~~f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\left|f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\epsilon)n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-C\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle{~~~}-\epsilon n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-2(1-\epsilon)n\gamma

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{5}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2},

where the last line is due to
\epsilon\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}/(\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}+4\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}})\leq 0.2
,
1-\epsilon\geq 0.8
,
n\geq C_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})
and phase one region constraint
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq 8C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1/2}\gamma
. We could prove upper bound of
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}
in a similar fashion,

\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}}.

Phase Two

First consider lower bound of
f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
. Follow Section
B
and we obtain when
C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}\max\{\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{n}},\epsilon\}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma
,

\displaystyle f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq(1-\epsilon)\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

Besides, same as phase one analyses, under event
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{o}
, it has

\displaystyle\big|f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\big|\leq\epsilon n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}+C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

Thus, the above two equations lead to

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\left|f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-f_{o}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\epsilon)\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\epsilon n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}C_{{\tiny u}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}-C\sqrt{\epsilon n\tilde{s}C_{{\tiny u}}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{C_{3}b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2},

where the last line uses
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}b_{0}\max\{\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{s}\log(2d/\tilde{s})}{n}},\epsilon\}
. Similar to Section
B
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega\cup\Pi\cup\Omega^{*}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{2}\leq C_{4}C_{{\tiny u}}\frac{n}{b_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
8

We only need to prove the following regularity properties.

Lemma 20
.

Assume
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
and
\{{\rm vec}({\mathbf{X}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfy Assumptions
2
and
1
, respectively. There exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},c_{1}>0
such that if
n\geq C_{1}rd_{1}C_{{\tiny u}}C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}
,
\epsilon\leq\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}/(4\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}+\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}})
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{1}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}\geq 8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{2}:=\left\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:C_{2}b_{0}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{{\tiny u}}}{C_{{\tiny l}}^{2}}}\cdot\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r}{n}},\epsilon\right\}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}<8\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}^{-1}}\gamma\right\},

then with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{1}rd_{1})-3\exp(-\sqrt{n}/\log n)
, the absolute loss
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|
satisfies Condition
1
:

1.

the rank-
r
restricted
two-phase sharpness
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{\ast})\geq\begin{cases}\frac{n}{4}\sqrt{C_{{\tiny l}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
\frac{n}{12b_{0}}C_{{\tiny l}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F}^{2},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2};\end{cases}

2.

the rank-r restricted
two-phase sub-gradient bound
with respect to
{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}
,

\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm F,r}\leq\begin{cases}2n\sqrt{C_{{\tiny u}}},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{1};\\
C_{3}nb_{1}^{-1}C_{{\tiny u}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{\ast}\|_{\rm F},&\textrm{ for }\ \ {\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{2},\end{cases}

where
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is any sub-gradient.

Proof of Lemma
20
would be a combination of absolute loss low-rank study in Lemma
6
and outlier analyses in Lemma
19
. We also study loss function of inliers and outliers seperately,

\displaystyle f_{i}({\mathbf{M}}):=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\right|,\quad f_{o}({\mathbf{M}}):=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{O}}}\left|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\right|.
